LATEST NEWS. THE WORMWOOD SCRUBBS MURDER

THE ACCUSED CONSTABLE BEFORE THE MAGISTRATES. In regard to the murder, an account of which is given on our inside pages, Police-cotstable George Samuel Cooke, twenty-seven, was charged at the West London Police Court, yesterday, with causing the death of Maud Smith, by striking her on the head at Wormwood Scrubbs on the morning of the 7th inst. Detective In- spector Morgan asked the magistrates to take evidence sufficient only to justify a remand. The accused, who is a tall man, entered the dock with a smile and look of recognition towards the police officers in court, and then folded his arms and assumed a careless air.

Harry Kimberley, a shepherd, said he was crossing Scrubbs early on Wednesday morning, and came upon a woman's dead body. One side of the face was covered with blood, and one side a the head was smashed in. The body was fifty yards from the road- way, and the clothing was scarcely disarranged at all. Witness touched the woman's hand, and finding it very cold immediately concluded she was dead. Witness called another man, and waited while the latter fetched a policeman. Mr. Jackson, police surgeon, said he was called, and found the body lying in a clot of blood. There were two fractures of the skull and two of the jaw, while the ball of the right eye was smashed. The pmt mortent showed extensive laceration and hemor- rhage on the brain. Witness had examined some police boots and trousers submitted to him. and found stains upon both ; he could not now state what the stains were. The wounds which caused time woman's death might be caused by kicks or blows from a blunt instrument. Detective-Inspector Morgan gave evidence that pri- soner went on duty at 9.45 on Tuesday night. Stains of blood were found on his boots and trousers, for which he could not account, except that on Thursday last week he cut his thumb.

===== 0002601_18930610_art0104.txt | included=true | included =====
The prisoner was remanded until next Thursday.

IMPERIAL PARLIAMENT.

FRIDAY. HOUSE OF COMMONS. . . .

The Speaker took the air at five minutes past two o'clock. THE WEAVER NAVIGATION BILL. . .

Mr. BROWNER moved that it be an Instruction to the Committee to inquire into and report on all matters connected with the constitution, powers, finance, and manavnnent of the Trustees of the Weaver Navigation. Ile said his instrucilon was now merely a formality. lie had had a conference with the President of the Board of Trade, who had announced his intention Of moving his amendment that it be an instruction to the commit- tee to inert in the bill a • clause requiring the trustees to come to Parliament within two years with such a Bill as might enable Parliament to deal with the whole question of the constitution and management of the trust. Ho was very much gratified to hear of that in- tention, because the amendment met all his (Mr. Brun- ner's) wishes. Be hal never had any wish to delay or defeat the Bill, and when it was pointed out to him that his motion would occupy thne involving the pre- panktion of evidence on behalf of the trustees he at once cordially accepted Mr. Musdella's proposal.— Mr. BRUNNER'S motion having been vribolrawn, Mr. MUNDELL' proposed hikinstruction in favour of a clause requiring the trustees to bring in a Bill within two years, so that the constitution of the Trust might be dealt with. 1, Mr. Fonwoon said the Bill had an imprirtant connec- tion with Liverpool, seeing that the exports of salt from Liverpool tormed a larger amount of the total than any_ other article. It was a wrong principle that the profit frein a great natural highway should go to ease the rates of any particular county. Mr. M'Lauxii on behalf of the other members for Cheshire, accepted the inetruction.—After some re- marks from Mr. Whiteley and Mr. Swipe, Mr. TOLLE- :maxim said he believed that there was at the present moment a motion on the books of the Trust in favour of the representation on it of the County Council. As to the surplus at the dues not one penny had been given for the 'relief of the rates without the sanction of Parliament. The instruction was then agreed to.

NTOTICE IS HEREBY GIVF,N, that the next ..1.11 GENERAL QUARTER SESSIONS of the Peace for the COUNTY OF CHESTER will be holden at the CASTLE OF CHESTER, in and for the said County, on MONDAY, the 28th day of June instant, at Half-past Eleven o'clock in the Forenoon, when the Grand Jury will be empannelled. The bearing of all Appeals, arising within the Hun- dreds ot Broxton, Eddisbury, Nantwich and Wirral, forming the Cheater District, and the trials of all pm- goners committed from such Hundreds, will take place at such Sessions. The hearing of all Appeals arising within tits Hundreds of Rucklow, Mac- clesfield, and Tiorthwich, forming the Knutsford District and the trimly of all prisoners committed from the last- mentioned Hundreds will take place at such Adjourned Sessions. The Grand and Petty Jury for the Knuts- ford District must be in attendance at the Court House, Knutsford, at the opening of the Court on WEDNESDAY, the 28th day of June instant, at Eleven o'clock in the Forenoon. REGINALD POTTS, Clerk of the Peace for the County of Chester. Chester, sth June, 1893.

SCIIOOL ATT ENDA SCE. . 1 ' The visitors had bean reported to the School Atten- dance Coinmitteis that during the month ending May , 19th, the number on the rohs was 3,700.. against 2,609- 'in the corresponding month last year. PRINCESS MAY AND THE' INSURED VICTORIAN. Several of the Victorians are down with influenza in, ' the temporary hospital at the Agricultural Hall in con- nection with the military tournament. The sergeant who broke his leg in two places in the musical ride has received a note from Princess May and a basket of flowers. It will be remembered that he continued after his accident to take part in ths ride, completing it all but the charge, for joining in which his compound frac- ture totally incapacitated him. TRADE RETURNS FOR: MAY. THE EBBING, - TIDE CHECKED. The Board of Trade retur- ns, issued on Thursday, show that the imports tor the month ended 31st May, were £38,836,951, against £35,035,738 for the corresponding , month last year, being an increase of £1,801,213 ; the I exports for May were £17.822,480, against £17,783,969, being an increase of £38,491 compared with last year. The imports for the five months ended May were £165,851,921, against £180415,394. in 1892. showing a decrease of £14,163,467. The exports for the five months amounted to £88,992,669., against £93790,684 in the corresponding period last year, being a decrease of \, £4,798,015.

Fegan (Boys' Home, Southwark), give their unqualified endorsement as to its value in piles and rheumatism, as well as in burns, bruises, cuts (they occur in my family); also for colds on the chest, toothache, and all akin diseases ? THAT FACT IS THE BEST PROOF OF VALUE. WE DO NOT FEAR COMPETITION, and we court comparison, for our position is trapreguahle. WHY BECAUSE we buy far " cash " and sell for " cash" many million pounds of Tea every year; so we can sell and do sell every pound for the smallest profit. We deal in TEA ONLY. It is plain Indeed that a firm which devotes all its attention to Tea alone must give the Public the best possible value In Tea. Our gigantic business has been established twenty-three years, our reputation is so high, and we have altogether so much at stake that we are thus bound by the best and surest of all guaran- tees to do the utmost passible for the Public and for you. WILL YOU NOW TRY THE TEA ITSELF? It Is sold in One-pound, Half-pound, and Quarter., pound Packets. Each is prepared and sealed by us at our own Warehouses, and bears our Signature and celebrated Trade Mark of the double-tr:angle or six-pointed star. OUR AGENTS draw their supplies constantly from us, and they sell exactly the same Tea at the same prices as at our celebrated warehouses. 11/-, 1/2, 1/4, 1/8, 2/.-, 2/4 or 2/8 per lb. WE ARE GLAD to receive applications for Agencies In this District from energetic trades- people. Selling our Tea is already a regular annuity to thousands of enterprising men and women. BROOKE, BOND, & CO., LIMITED, 17, PICCADILLY, MANCHESTER, AND AT LEEDS, BRADFORD, AND LOSLN.
